# LLP 3000.000.000.002: Declarative release backfills

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, storage, release adoption, staging clock, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (MIG)
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Revised:** 2026-10-19
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Staging](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md)

## 1. Commission

Implementer: MIG, 2026-10-19. Wave 17's fifty changes require constant,
conditional, derived, enum, string-split and point-lookup transformations.
Declare these in Quarry, with no public migration mutation, job or JavaScript.
REL5 owns release selection/restoration and old-client compatibility; SEC3 owns
security checks. No new version, publication or push authority is implied.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Continuous carry-forward keeps the
same normalized definition; changing it under the same name refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-4`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New/inactive declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot and
catch up changed old inputs. A native-image exception requires absent historical
inputs and valid, physically present outputs introduced after the predecessor;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Evidence decode is preflighted against
the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.

## 4. Staging clock

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and holds a stopped-store lock through inspection.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

## 5. Evidence

Tests cover all fifty historical shapes, no-op/restart, v1 writes during
rollback, same-column enum reversal, edited-output refusal, missing lookup,
invalid output, uniqueness failure, deletion/id reuse, generation reset and
hot/cold/preview equivalence. The guide's persistent production recipe uses
real users and operator role setup, tests tightened privacy and declares its
invariant, rolls back and forward with business assertions. Its exact fences
run against the fresh packed tarball. Reproduce five copied bakeoff lanes,
record actual line/time deltas. Run required crate/client/differential/Linux,
Mac Studio clock tests, refs, budget and root checks.

## 6. Reviews

Round 1: [Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r1.muse.txt) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r1.codex.txt) returned NOT READY.
This revision specifies historical eligibility, exact preimages, lookup changes,
phase order, immutable declaration identities, reference/asset restrictions,
format/generation fences and bounded bridge repairs. It retains strict conflict
refusal rather than Muse's suggested silent edited-row preservation: preserving
a different same-column value under old semantics could change business meaning.
The author explicitly permits rollback refusal with a reason.

The [fifty-shape executable inventory](../snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/tests/backfills/wave17.json)
tracks every line of `~/sb4-loop/wave17/changes.txt`. Exact source solutions are
read-only under `~/sb4-loop/wave17/apps/NNN-snapback-SLUG/`; MIG's copied-lane
reproduction and measured results are recorded in its report.


Round 2: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r2.codex.txt) found four
material cases: dormant membership double application, late narrowed-row
restoration, historical lookup cardinality, and wall-time hot activation. These
now have focused regression coverage and the explicit phase/time rules above.
[Muse attempt](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r2.muse.txt) was unavailable with a
provider subscription-quota error; no verdict is inferred. A later fresh round
reviews the corrected candidate independently.

Round 3: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r3.codex.txt) found
identity output admission, dropped historical reference invalidation, source
expiry retirement, pruned-membership re-entry and deleted-lifetime pruning.
Admission now rejects identity; consumed invalid historical references refuse
regardless of candidate visibility. Every backfill source is retired against
its original expiry before evaluation. Pruned membership forbids re-entry when
historical evidence is unavailable, and prune forgets evidence for lifetimes it
deletes. Each case has a focused regression. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 4: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r4.codex.txt) found
cold activation dropping its persisted time floor when removing the last
`not before`, and absent optional outputs qualifying as a native image.
Activation now clamps all phases to the existing durable floor regardless
of candidate boundaries, resampling under the writer; rebuilds use the same
floor. Native-image exceptions require physically present output fields.
Both cases have regressions. Muse again returned subscription quota exhaustion.

Round 5: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r5.codex.txt) found
new declarations skipping restored outputs, dropped historical arguments,
multiple-declaration snapshot reconstruction, and preview preimage counts.
The corrected implementation records original adoption cohorts, reconstructs
their shared source images/indexes, recovers dropped inputs, stages all cohorts
before applying patches, and lists preimages separately. Focused regressions
cover simultaneous and later declarations, lookups, missing optional arguments,
and new-name transforms. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 6: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r6.codex.txt) found
partial-table restoration omitting a carried declaration's historical image,
and preview using HTTP loopback dialability as an ownership test. Cohort
reconstruction now includes every current declaration in the cohort while
only eligible transformations run. Preview probes the existing owner lock,
holds stopped-store exclusion, and otherwise obtains time privately. Regressions
cover carried parents/restored children and both offset signs on explicit
non-loopback binds. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

//! Historical row transforms, separate from application-callable programs.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#2-language — the compiler and raw-schema boundary share a closed expression fragment.
use super::*;
use crate::ir::{Builtin, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Stmt};
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Backfill {
    pub table: String,
    pub name: String,
    pub inputs: BTreeMap<String, ColumnType>,
    pub outputs: BTreeSet<String>,
    pub program: Program,
}
impl Backfill {
    pub fn key(&self) -> String { digest(&self.normalized()) }
    pub fn normalized(&self) -> Self {
        // Locations are diagnostic metadata, never transformation identity.
        let mut normalized = self.clone();
        fn expr(e: &mut Expr) {
            match e {
                Expr::Get(r) | Expr::Exists(r) => { r.site.clear(); for e in &mut r.key { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Scan(r) => {
                    r.site.clear();
                    for k in &mut r.prefix { if let crate::ir::IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(e)=k {expr(e);} }
                    if let Some(e)=&mut r.filter {expr(e);}
                    if let Some(r)=&mut r.range {for e in [&mut r.gt,&mut r.gte,&mut r.lt,&mut r.lte].into_iter().flatten(){expr(e);}}
                }
                Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => expr(base),
                Expr::Array(es) | Expr::Builtin { args: es, .. } => { for e in es { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Object(fs) => { for e in fs.values_mut() { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Shape { base, fields } => { expr(base); for e in fields.values_mut() { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expr(left); expr(right); }
                Expr::Block { body, value } => { statements(body); expr(value); }
                _ => (),
            }
        }
        fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt]) {
            for s in body {
                match s {
                    Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr(value),
                    Stmt::Require { condition, site, .. } => { *site=None; expr(condition); },
                    Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => { expr(condition); statements(then_body); statements(else_body); }
                    _ => (),
                }
            }
        }
        statements(&mut normalized.program.body);
        normalized
    }
    pub fn admit(&self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), String> {
        let table = schema.table(&self.table).ok_or("backfill names an absent table")?;
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || schema.maintain_for(&self.table).is_some() { return Err("backfill requires an authoritative durable table".into()); }
        if self.program.kind != ProgramKind::Query || self.program.native || self.outputs.is_empty() { return Err("backfill must produce a nonempty object patch".into()); }
        for c in &self.outputs {
            let kind = table.columns.get(c).ok_or_else(||format!("backfill output {}.{c} is not declared",self.table))?;
            if c=="id" || table.engine_field(c) || table.maintained.contains_key(c) || table.expire.as_ref()==Some(c) || table.not_before.as_ref()==Some(c) || !scalar(kind) {
                return Err(format!("backfill output {}.{c} must be an authoritative scalar, not an asset, lifetime or captured field",self.table));
            }
        }
        if !body(&self.program.body,schema) { return Err("backfill expressions allow deterministic scalar transforms and unique point lookups only; no viewer, clock, ids, scans, loops or writes".into()); }
        Ok(())
    }
}
fn scalar(k: &ColumnType) -> bool {
    match k {
        ColumnType::Optional(k) => scalar(k),
        ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. } | ColumnType::List { .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { .. } => false,
        _ => true,
    }
}
fn body(stmts: &[Stmt], s: &Schema) -> bool {
    stmts.iter().all(|stmt| match stmt {
        Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr(value,s),
        Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr(condition,s),
        Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => expr(condition,s) && body(then_body,s) && body(else_body,s),
        _ => false,
    })
}
fn expr(e: &Expr, s: &Schema) -> bool {
    match e {
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Variable(_) => true,
        Expr::Input(_) | Expr::Merge(_) => false,
        Expr::Scan(r) => r.existence && r.after.is_none() && r.before.is_none() && r.prefix.iter().all(|k| k.scalar().is_some_and(|e|expr(e,s))) && r.filter.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|expr(e,s)) && r.range.as_ref().is_none_or(|r|[&r.gt,&r.gte,&r.lt,&r.lte].iter().all(|e|e.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|expr(e,s)))),
        Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => expr(base,s),
        Expr::Array(es) => es.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Object(es) => es.values().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Shape { base, fields } => expr(base,s) && fields.values().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => expr(left,s) && expr(right,s),
        Expr::Block { body: stmts, value } => body(stmts,s) && expr(value,s),
        Expr::Get(r) | Expr::Exists(r) => s.table(&r.table).is_some_and(|t| t.index(&r.index).is_some_and(|i|i.unique && i.is_plain() && i.components.len()==r.key.len())) && r.key.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Builtin { builtin, args } => matches!(builtin,
            Builtin::RequireUncapped | Builtin::ProjectFields { .. } | Builtin::OptionalField { .. } | Builtin::Len | Builtin::Count | Builtin::Slice | Builtin::Concat | Builtin::Lower | Builtin::Trim | Builtin::Text(_) | Builtin::Sha256 | Builtin::First | Builtin::Last | Builtin::At | Builtin::Min | Builtin::Max | Builtin::Divide { .. } | Builtin::Civil(_) | Builtin::Calendar { .. }
        ) && args.len()==builtin.arity() && args.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
    }
}
//! Release backfills use the same activation transaction as retention and indexes.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#3-adoption-and-reversibility — stage every result before mutating the input snapshot.
use super::*;
use crate::{schema::{backfill::Backfill, materialize_optional_fields}, engine::{Context, runner::Runner}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
pub(crate) const VERSION: &str = "snapback4-release-store-4";
fn refusal(message: impl Into<String>) -> RuntimeError { RuntimeError::Structural { code:"E_ADOPT", message:message.into() } }
fn present(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
fn enable(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_evidence(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,inputs TEXT NOT NULL,before_image TEXT NOT NULL,after_image TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,epoch INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_pruned(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_definitions(digest TEXT PRIMARY KEY,definition TEXT NOT NULL);")?;
    if !tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM pragma_table_info('_backfill_evidence') WHERE name='epoch')",[],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
        tx.conn().execute_batch("ALTER TABLE _backfill_evidence ADD COLUMN epoch INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;")?;
    }
    tx.set_meta("version", VERSION)
}
#[derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
struct Evidence { #[serde(default)] epoch:i64, active:bool, inputs: Value, before: BTreeMap<String,Value>, after: BTreeMap<String,Value> }
fn evidence(tx: &Tx<'_>, t:i64,id:&str,key:&str) -> Result<Option<Evidence>,RuntimeError> {
    let bytes:Option<i64>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT octet_length(inputs)+octet_length(before_image)+octet_length(after_image) FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
    let mut admission=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);admission.heap(bytes.unwrap_or(0).max(0) as u64 * 64 + 8192)?;
    let value:Option<(String,String,String,bool,i64)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT inputs,before_image,after_image,active,epoch FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?,r.get(3)?,r.get(4)?))).optional()?;
    value.map(|(inputs,before,after,active,epoch)|Ok(Evidence {epoch,active,inputs:decode(&inputs)?,before:decode(&before)?,after:decode(&after)?})).transpose()
}
fn decode<T:serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(s:&str)->Result<T,RuntimeError>{serde_json::from_str(s).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))}
fn save(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,key:&str,e:&Evidence,active:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_evidence(t,id,definition,inputs,before_image,after_image,active,epoch) VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7,?8) ON CONFLICT(t,id,definition) DO UPDATE SET inputs=excluded.inputs,before_image=excluded.before_image,after_image=excluded.after_image,active=excluded.active,epoch=excluded.epoch",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&e.inputs),canonical_json(&e.before),canonical_json(&e.after),active,e.epoch])?;
    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key])?;
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn delete(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if present(tx)? {
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
    } Ok(())
}
// Never archive reference/asset preimages without lifetime evidence. New reference
// fields are safe: subsequent retirement is owned by the ordinary dormant ledger.
fn protected(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind {Optional(k)=>protected(k),Ref{..}|Image{..}|Video{..}|File{..}|List{..}|ListRange{..}=>true,_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn transition(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:Option<&Schema>,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,now:i64,report:&mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if next.backfills.is_empty() && previous.is_none_or(|s|s.backfills.is_empty()) && !present(tx)? { return Ok(()); }
    enable(tx)?;
    for fill in &next.backfills { fill.admit(next).map_err(refusal)?; }
    // A name is immutable within a continuous lineage. Replacing a transform
    // deliberately goes through an old-source/bridge transition first.
    if let Some(old)=previous {for f in &next.backfills {if old.backfills.iter().any(|p|p.table==f.table && p.name==f.name && p.key()!=f.key()) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} changed; use a new name in a bridge release rather than replacing historical meaning",f.table,f.name)));}}}
    for fill in &next.backfills {
        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_definitions VALUES(?1,?2)",params![fill.key(),canonical_json(fill)])?;
    }
    let next_keys:BTreeSet<_>=next.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key).collect();
    let definitions:Vec<Backfill>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition FROM _backfill_definitions WHERE digest IN (SELECT definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 UNION SELECT definition FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE active=1) ORDER BY digest")?.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))?.map(|s|decode(&s?)).collect::<Result<_,RuntimeError>>()?;
    for fill in definitions.iter().rev().filter(|f|!next_keys.contains(&f.key())) {
        let Some(table)=next.table(&fill.table) else {continue};
        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
            let Some(e)=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)? else {
                let pruned:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3 AND active=1)",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0))?;
                if pruned && fill.outputs.iter().any(|c|table.columns.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,"rollback preimage was explicitly pruned; select a bridge preserving current data or restore a backup");}
                return Ok(())
            };
            if !e.active {return Ok(())}
            let fields=match &mut row {Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=>f,_=>return Err(refusal("invalid stored row"))};
            let mut changed=false;
            for c in &fill.outputs {if table.columns.contains_key(c) {
                let current=fields.get(c);let before=e.before.get(c);let after=e.after.get(c);
                if current!=after && current!=before {bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} changed after backfill {}; keep a bridge and repair through an authorized mutation before rollback",fill.table,c,fill.name));return Ok(())}
                if let Some(value)=before {fields.insert(c.clone(),value.clone());}else{fields.remove(c);}
                changed=true;
            }}
            if changed {tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;count+=1;}
            save(tx,t,id,&key,&e,false)
        })?;
        if bad.count>0 {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} rollback: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
        if count>0 {report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} reversed on {count} {}",fill.table,fill.name,if count==1 {"row"}else{"rows"}));tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;}
    }
    let old_keys:BTreeSet<_>=previous.into_iter().flat_map(|s|s.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key)).collect();
    let restored_tables:BTreeSet<i64>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT n.t FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=n.t AND r.id=n.id)")?.query_map([],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    let entering:Vec<_>=next.backfills.iter().filter(|f|!old_keys.contains(&f.key()) || tx.num(&f.table).is_ok_and(|t|restored_tables.contains(&t))).collect();
    if entering.is_empty(){return Ok(())}
    let populated = entering.iter().try_fold(false, |any,fill| -> Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
        Ok(any || tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE t=?1)",[tx.num(&fill.table)?],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?)
    })?;
    if !populated {return Ok(())}
    let mut source=previous.cloned().unwrap_or_default();source.tables=sources.clone();source.backfills.clear();
    // Point lookup indexes are rebuilt against the assembled source images,
    // including restored rows, before any output patch is applied.
    let mut reads=BTreeSet::new();
    for f in &entering {collect_reads(&f.program.body,&mut reads);}
    for (name,index) in &reads {
        let wanted=next.table(name).and_then(|t|t.index(&index)).ok_or_else(||refusal("backfill lookup index absent"))?;
        let old_table=source.tables.get_mut(name).ok_or_else(||refusal(format!("backfill lookup source {name} has no historical definition")))?;
        if !wanted.is_plain() || wanted.columns().iter().any(|c|c!="id" && !old_table.columns.contains_key(c)) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill lookup {name}.{index} requires predecessor fields; use an indexed bridge")))}
        old_table.indexes.insert(index.clone(),wanted.clone());
    }
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _backfill_pending(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,patch TEXT NOT NULL,evidence TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _backfill_originals(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,data TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    let entering_keys:BTreeSet<_>=entering.iter().map(|f|f.key()).collect();
    let mut epochs=BTreeSet::from([None]);
    for key in &entering_keys {
        let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT epoch FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE definition=?1 AND active=1 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_backfill_evidence.t AND r.id=_backfill_evidence.id)")?;
        for epoch in q.query_map([key],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0))? {epochs.insert(Some(epoch?));}
    }
    let fresh_epoch=tx.meta("generation")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<i64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0).saturating_add(1);
    for epoch in epochs {
        restore_snapshot(tx)?;
        if let Some(epoch)=epoch { historical_snapshot(tx,epoch,&next_keys)?; }
        rebuild_reads(tx,&source,&reads)?;
    for fill in &entering {

        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let table=&next.tables[&fill.table];let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
            if old_keys.contains(&key) && !tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",params![t,id],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {return Ok(())}
            let prior=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)?;
            if prior.as_ref().filter(|e|e.active).map(|e|e.epoch)!=epoch {return Ok(())}
            let original:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM temp._backfill_originals WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
            let current:Value=if let Some(text)=original {decode(&text)?}else{row.clone()};
            if prior.is_none() && tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3)",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
                bad.add(id,"historical backfill evidence was explicitly pruned; cannot safely re-enter this declaration; use a new bridge or restore a backup");return Ok(())
            }
            for c in fill.inputs.keys() {
                if tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND invalid=1)",params![t,id,c],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
                    bad.add(id,&format!("historical reference {}.{c} lost its parent lifetime",fill.table));return Ok(())
                }
            }
            if let Some(table)=sources.get(&fill.table){materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);}
            let historical=row.clone();
            let mut args=BTreeMap::new();
            for (c,kind) in &fill.inputs {
                let value=historical.field(c).cloned().ok();
                if crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,kind,value.as_ref()).is_err() {
                    // Native rows can survive a return through an older source;
                    // they have no historical input, but retain a valid v2 image.
                    if prior.is_none() && previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_some_and(|old|fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|!old.columns.contains_key(c))) && fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|current.field(c).is_ok() && crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],current.field(c).ok()).is_ok())
                        && tx.conn().query_row("SELECT NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 UNION ALL SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",params![t,id],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {return Ok(())}
                    bad.add(id,&format!("historical input {}.{c} is absent or does not fit {kind}; use a typed bridge to repair it",fill.table));return Ok(())
                }
                args.insert(c.clone(),value.unwrap_or(Value::Null));
            }
            let inputs=Value::Row(args.clone().into());
            let mut context=Context::system().at(now);context.args=args;
            let mut runner=Runner::new(tx,&source,context,Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort),0);
            let patch=match runner.run(&fill.program) {Ok(Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f))=>f.into_iter().collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(),Ok(_)=>{bad.add(id,"backfill did not return an object");return Ok(())},Err(e)=>{bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}};
            if runner.read_cut().is_some() {bad.add(id,"historical lookup did not complete; reduce per-row lookup work before adoption");return Ok(())}
            if patch.keys().cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()!=fill.outputs {return Err(refusal("backfill returned undeclared output fields"))}
            let fields=current.as_object()?;let mut before=prior.as_ref().filter(|e|e.active).map_or_else(BTreeMap::new,|e|e.before.clone());let mut desired=patch.clone();
            for (c,value) in &patch {
                if let Err(e)=crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],Some(value)) {bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}
                if let Some(kind)=sources.get(&fill.table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(c)) {if protected(kind) && prior.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|e.before.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,&format!("cannot overwrite historical reference or asset {}.{c}; derive into a new column",fill.table));return Ok(())}}
                if !prior.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.active) && (previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(c)) || (previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_none() && sources.get(&fill.table).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(c)))) {
                    if let Some(v)=historical.field(c).ok(){before.insert(c.clone(),v.clone());}
                }
                if let Some(prior)=&prior {
                    let deps_same=prior.inputs==inputs && prior.after==patch;
                    let current=fields.get(c);
                    let original=prior.before.get(c);
                    if deps_same && (prior.active || original.is_none()) && current.is_some() {
                        // Added columns retain edits under v1's dormant storage.
                        desired.insert(c.clone(),current.unwrap().clone());
                    } else if !deps_same && current!=prior.after.get(c) && current!=original {
                        bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} was edited and its historical inputs changed; keep a bridge and resolve the conflict",fill.table,c));return Ok(())
                    }
                }
            }
            let e=Evidence{epoch:epoch.unwrap_or(fresh_epoch),active:true,inputs,before,after:patch};
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO temp._backfill_pending VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5)",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&desired),canonical_json(&e)])?;count+=1;
            Ok(())
        })?;
        if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {}: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
        if count>0{report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} transformed {count} {}",fill.table,fill.name,if count==1 {"row"}else{"rows"}));}
    }
    }
    restore_snapshot(tx)?;
    // Stream patches; avoid retaining the whole transformed population in RAM.
    let mut stmt=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT t,id,definition,patch,evidence FROM temp._backfill_pending ORDER BY t,id,definition")?;
    let mut rows=stmt.query([])?;
    while let Some(r)=rows.next()? {
        let t:i64=r.get(0)?;let id:String=r.get(1)?;let key:String=r.get(2)?;
        let patch:BTreeMap<String,Value>=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(3)?)?;let e:Evidence=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(4)?)?;
        let data:String=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0))?;let mut row:Value=decode(&data)?;
        if let Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=&mut row {f.extend(patch);}
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
        save(tx,t,&id,&key,&e,true)?;
    }
    drop(rows);drop(stmt);
    tx.conn().execute_batch("DROP TABLE temp._backfill_pending; DROP TABLE temp._backfill_originals;")?;
    // The source-index rebuild and every transformed dependency require full
    // candidate key/cap/maintain validation, even when its declaration is equal.
    tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;
    Ok(())
}
fn collect_reads(body:&[crate::ir::Stmt],out:&mut BTreeSet<(String,String)>){
    use crate::ir::{Expr,Stmt};
    fn expr(e:&Expr,out:&mut BTreeSet<(String,String)>){match e{
        Expr::Scan(r)=>{out.insert((r.table.clone(),r.index.clone()));for k in &r.prefix{if let Some(e)=k.scalar(){expr(e,out);}}if let Some(range)=&r.range {for e in [&range.gt,&range.gte,&range.lt,&range.lte].into_iter().flatten(){expr(e,out);}}if let Some(e)=&r.filter{expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Get(r)|Expr::Exists(r)=>{out.insert((r.table.clone(),r.index.clone()));for e in &r.key{expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Field{base,..}|Expr::Unary{value:base,..}=>expr(base,out),Expr::Array(es)|Expr::Builtin{args:es,..}=>{for e in es{expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Object(fs)=>{for e in fs.values(){expr(e,out);}},Expr::Shape{base,fields}=>{expr(base,out);for e in fields.values(){expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Binary{left,right,..}=>{expr(left,out);expr(right,out);},Expr::Block{body,value}=>{collect_reads(body,out);expr(value,out);},_=>()
    }}
    for s in body{match s{Stmt::Let{value,..}|Stmt::Const{value,..}|Stmt::Assign{value,..}|Stmt::Return(value)=>expr(value,out),Stmt::Require{condition,..}=>expr(condition,out),Stmt::If{condition,then_body,else_body}=>{expr(condition,out);collect_reads(then_body,out);collect_reads(else_body,out);},_=>()}}
}

// Prune forgets historical values, retaining only the fact that exact reversal
// is no longer available. No tombstone can be queried by an app.
pub(super) fn prune(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if present(tx)? {tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_backfill_evidence.t AND r.id=_backfill_evidence.id); DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_backfill_pruned.t AND r.id=_backfill_pruned.id); INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_pruned SELECT t,id,definition,active FROM _backfill_evidence; DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence;")?;}
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn inventory_sql(conn:&Connection)->Result<&'static str,RuntimeError>{
    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
    Ok(if exists {" UNION ALL SELECT c.name||'.backfill#'||substr(e.definition,1,12),'backfill',c.name,e.definition FROM _backfill_evidence e JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=e.t GROUP BY e.t,e.definition "}else{""})
}

pub(super) fn has_evidence(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{present(tx)}
pub(super) fn columns(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema)->Result<BTreeMap<String,BTreeSet<String>>,RuntimeError>{
    let mut out:BTreeMap<String,BTreeSet<String>>=BTreeMap::new();
    for f in &next.backfills {out.entry(f.table.clone()).or_default().extend(f.outputs.iter().cloned());}
    if present(tx)? {
        let mut statement=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition FROM _backfill_definitions WHERE digest IN (SELECT definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 UNION SELECT definition FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE active=1)")?;
        for text in statement.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))? {
            let f:Backfill=decode(&text?)?;out.entry(f.table).or_default().extend(f.outputs);
        }
    }
    Ok(out)
}
fn validate_lookup(tx:&Tx<'_>,ti:i64,prefix:usize,table:&str,index:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    let mut stmt=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT k,id FROM i WHERE ti=?1 ORDER BY k")?;let mut rows=stmt.query([ti])?;
    let mut prior=None;let mut first=String::new();let mut duplicate=false;let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();
    while let Some(row)=rows.next()? {
        let key:Vec<u8>=row.get(0)?;let id:String=row.get(1)?;let mut group=crate::key::decode(&key).ok_or_else(||refusal("invalid historical lookup key"))?;group.truncate(prefix);
        if group.contains(&Value::Null){continue;}
        if prior.as_ref()==Some(&group) {
            if !duplicate {bad.add(&first,"duplicate historical lookup key");duplicate=true;}
            bad.add(&id,"duplicate historical lookup key");
        }else{prior=Some(group);first=id;duplicate=false;}
    }
    if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("historical lookup {table}.{index} is not unique: {}; prepare a unique bridge before deriving values",bad.describe())))}
    Ok(())
}

// Each original adoption cohort saw one snapshot. Re-entry restores that same
// view across all its declarations; later cohorts read earlier cohorts' outputs.
fn restore_snapshot(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=(SELECT o.data FROM temp._backfill_originals o WHERE o.t=r.t AND o.id=r.id) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM temp._backfill_originals o WHERE o.t=r.t AND o.id=r.id)",[])?;Ok(())
}
fn historical_snapshot(tx:&Tx<'_>,epoch:i64,keys:&BTreeSet<String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT t,id,definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 AND epoch=?1 ORDER BY t,id,definition")?;
    let mut rows=q.query([epoch])?;
    while let Some(r)=rows.next()? {
        let t:i64=r.get(0)?;let id:String=r.get(1)?;let key:String=r.get(2)?;if !keys.contains(&key){continue;}
        let data:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
        let Some(data)=data else {continue};
        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._backfill_originals SELECT t,id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
        let mut row:Value=decode(&data)?;let e=evidence(tx,t,&id,&key)?.unwrap();
        let fields=match &mut row {Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=>f,_=>return Err(refusal("invalid historical row"))};
        if let Value::Row(inputs)|Value::Map(inputs)=e.inputs {for(c,value)in inputs {fields.entry(c).or_insert(value);}}
        for c in e.after.keys() {if !e.before.contains_key(c) {fields.remove(c);}}
        for(c,value)in e.before {fields.insert(c,value);}
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
    } Ok(())
}
fn rebuild_reads(tx:&Tx<'_>,source:&Schema,reads:&BTreeSet<(String,String)>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    for (name,index) in reads {
        let old=&source.tables[name].indexes[index];let ti=tx.num(&format!("{name}.{index}"))?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti=?1",[ti])?;
        super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(name)?,|id,mut row|{
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&source.tables[name]);
            for key in index_keys(old,&row,id){tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO i(ti,k,id) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![ti,key,id])?;}Ok(())
        })?;
        if old.unique {validate_lookup(tx,ti,old.components.len(),name,index)?;}
    } Ok(())
}

//! Candidate activation: project rows, validate invariants, build indexes
//! and derived rows, then publish the semantic generation in one commit.
// @ref LLP 3000.000#implementation-follow-up — one atomic activation boundary

pub(super) use super::activation_walk::walk;
use super::activation_walk::walk_metered;
use super::*;
use crate::ir::Program;
use crate::schema::{Constraint, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::value::digest;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

pub(super) type Identity = (Option<String>, Option<String>, Option<String>, Option<String>,
);

pub(super) fn identity(conn: &Connection) -> Result<Identity, RuntimeError> {
    let mut statement = conn.prepare_cached("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1")?;
    let mut get = |key| statement.query_row([key], |row| row.get(0)).optional();
    Ok((get("schema_hash")?, get("activation_hash")?, get("generation")?, get("device:generation")?,
    ))
}

impl Store {
    /// Install a device's schema. Server engines also supply their programs
    /// and derived-state builder through `activate` below.
    pub fn install(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.activate(schema, None, false, |_, _| Ok(())).map(|_| ())
    }

    /// A device can discard an obsolete partition while atomically caching
    /// its replacement backend and reset watermark. It never migrates rows
    /// whose authority belongs to a previous server generation.
    pub fn install_device(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.install_device_checked(schema, metadata, reset, &BTreeMap::new())
    }

    pub fn install_device_checked(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.install_device_checked_with(schema, metadata, reset, expected, |_| Ok(()))
    }

    /// Device-owned sidecar changes share the schema installation transaction.
    pub fn install_device_checked_with(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>, prepare: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.install_device_checked_with_finish(schema, metadata, reset, expected, prepare, |_| {
            Ok(())
        })
    }

    /// Opening recovery and its final reads must finish before installation commits.
    pub fn install_device_checked_with_finish<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>, prepare: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<R, RuntimeError> {
        let mut result = None;
        self.activate(schema, None, reset, |tx, _| {
            for (key, value) in expected {
                let held: Option<String> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1", [key], |row| {
                        row.get(0)
                    }).optional()?;
                if &held != value { return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the backend adoption capture is obsolete; retry sync".into())); }
            }
            prepare(tx)?;
            for (key, value) in metadata {
                // A device metadata namespace reset shares backend installation's transaction.
                if key.ends_with(':') {
                    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key >= ?1 AND key < ?2", params![key, format!("{key}\u{10ffff}")])?;
                } else { tx.set_meta(key, value)?; }
            }
            if reset { tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _device_base; DELETE FROM _device_snapshot;")?; }
            result = Some(finish(tx)?);
            Ok(())
        })?;
        Ok(result.expect("activation ran opening recovery"))
    }

    pub(crate) fn activate(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.activate_at(schema, programs, reset, crate::now_ms(), |tx, changed, _| build(tx, changed))
    }

    pub(crate) fn activate_at(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        // One-shot device preparation is never retried or given a larger cache.
        self.activate_transaction(schema, programs, reset, now, true, build)
    }

    pub(crate) fn activate_at_with_launch<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, allow_launch: bool, check: impl Fn(&Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, mut build: impl FnMut(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl Fn(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<(u64, R), RuntimeError> {
        let budget = if programs.is_some() { super::activation_cache::configured_mib()? } else { 0 };
        // The server builder owns only transaction-local work. Recreate all of
        // it on retry, with the same schema/programs/activation time.
        self.with_activation_cache(budget, |store| {
            store.activate_transaction_with(schema, programs, reset, now, allow_launch, &check, &mut build, &finish)
        })
    }

    pub(super) fn activate_transaction(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, allow_launch: bool, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.activate_transaction_with(schema, programs, reset, now, allow_launch, |_| Ok(()), build, |_| Ok(())).map(|(generation, ())| generation)
    }

    fn activate_transaction_with<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, allow_launch: bool, check: impl FnOnce(&Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,

            if ordered {
                let started = std::time::Instant::now();
                tx.conn().execute_batch("INSERT INTO main.i(ti, k, id) SELECT ti, k, id FROM temp._adoption_index ORDER BY ti, k; DROP TABLE temp._adoption_index;")?;
                report.detail("ordered index insertion", started);
            }
        }
        report.phase("project and index", &mut phase);
        // Upgrade old stores and rebuild only when the schema changes. Counts
        // participate in the same activation transaction as rows and indexes.
        if cap_counts && (population_changed || tx.meta("cap_counts_schema")?.as_deref() != Some(&schema_hash)) {
            tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _cap_counts;")?;
            for (name, table) in schema.tables.iter().filter(|(_,t)| t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
                if table.is_expiring() && table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { .. })) {
                    walk(&tx, catalog[name], |_, row| {
                        tx.update_cap_counts(name, table, None, Some(&row)).map(|_| ())
                    })?;
                }
            }
            tx.set_meta("cap_counts_schema", &schema_hash)?;
        }
        report.phase("cap counts", &mut phase);
        build(&tx, changed, &mut report)?;
        if rebuilding || reset || tx.meta(super::time_audience::PUBLICATION_STATE_KEY)?.as_deref() != Some("1") {
            tx.rebuild_time_audiences(schema, now)?;
        }
        report.phase("derived rebuild", &mut phase);
        if rebuilding && programs.is_some() && super::dormant::enabled(&tx)? {
            super::adopt_refusals::candidate(&tx,schema,if population_changed{None}else{previous.as_ref()},true)?;
        }
        if rebuilding {
            for (name, table) in schema.tables.iter().filter(|(_,t)| t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
                let plan = &plans[name];
                // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — physical reuse cannot exempt rebuilt rows from unchanged invariants.
                let rebuilt = schema.maintain_for(name).is_some_and(|m| {
                    previous.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| crate::successor::maintain_rebuild_needed(old, schema, m))
                });
                if plan.project || rebuilt {
                walk(&tx, catalog[name], |_, mut row| {
                    materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                    if programs.is_none() { if let Value::Row(fields) = &mut row { fields.remove("pending"); } }
                    crate::engine::validate::validate_maintained_cache(schema, name, &row)?;
                    for (column, kind) in &table.columns { validate_assets(&tx, kind, row.field(column).ok())?; }
                    Ok(())
                })?;
                }
                for (index_name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(name, i)| {
                    i.unique && (plan.project || rebuilt || plan.indexes.contains(*name))
                }) {
                    validate_groups(&tx, name, index_name, index.components.len(), 1, true)?;
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.2 — a `set null` cap skips its null group; adding or removing that exemption re-validates the cap.
                let exemption_changed = previous.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| old.tables.get(name).is_some_and(|old| old.constraints.iter().any(|c|
                    matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { by, .. } if old.cap_exempts_null(by) != table.cap_exempts_null(by)))));
                for constraint in table.constraints.iter().filter(|_| plan.project || rebuilt || exemption_changed) {
                    if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint {
                        let (index_name, _) = table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, i)| i.is_plain() && i.columns().starts_with(by)).ok_or_else(|| {
                                RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{name} cap has no matching index"))
                            })?;
                        validate_groups(&tx, name, index_name, by.len(), u64::from(*max), table.cap_exempts_null(by))?;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        report.phase("validation", &mut phase);
        if rebuilding {
            // A removed asset home is retired in the same transaction as its
            // projection. The ordinary collector removes bytes after commit.
            let homes: Vec<(String, String)> = tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT home_table, home_column FROM _assets WHERE home_table IS NOT NULL AND released_at IS NULL")?.query_map([], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
            for (table, column) in homes {
                if schema.table(&table).and_then(|t| t.columns.get(&column)).is_none_or(|kind| !contains_asset_type(kind)) && !super::dormant::holds_home(&tx,&table,&column)? {
                    tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at = ?3 WHERE home_table = ?1 AND home_column = ?2 AND released_at IS NULL", params![table, column, crate::now_ms()])?;
                }
            }
        }
        let previous = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'generation'", [], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0)).optional()?.and_then(|s| s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
        let generation = if programs.is_some() && changed { previous.checked_add(1).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))? } else { previous.max(1) };
        tx.set_meta("schema_hash", &schema_hash)?;
        if let Some(hash) = &activation_hash {
            tx.set_meta("index_keys_validated", &format!("{}:{hash}", crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES))?;
        } else {
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'", [])?;
        }
        if let (Some(hash), Some(programs)) = (&activation_hash, programs) {
            tx.set_meta("activation_hash", hash)?;
            tx.set_meta("generation", &generation.to_string())?;
            if changed {
                tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _generations(generation, schema, programs, at) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, ?4)", params![i64::try_from(generation).map_err(|_| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))?, canonical_json(schema), canonical_json(programs), crate::now_ms()])?;
            }
            // Inspection-only metadata shares the successful activation's
            // transaction, but never its schema bytes, hash or generation.
            if let Some(indexes) = &schema.authored_indexes.0 {
                let record = crate::schema::inspection::Record {
                    generation, schema_hash, indexes: indexes.clone(),
                };
                tx.set_meta(crate::schema::inspection::META_KEY, &canonical_json(&record))?;
            }
        }
        let after = identity(tx.conn())?;
        super::live_activation::finalize(&tx)?;
        let result = finish(&tx)?;
        super::live_activation::cleanup(&tx)?;
        report.phase("metadata", &mut phase);
        tx.commit()?;
        report.phase("commit", &mut phase);
        // Reopening an unchanged backend must not announce a new adoption.
        self.adoption_report = if changed || reset { report } else { Default::default() };
        // Historical ordinals stay in _catalog, but no runtime transaction can address memory tables.
        catalog.retain(|name,_| {
            !schema.tables.iter().any(|(table,t)| {
                t.ephemeral.is_some() && (name == table || name.starts_with(&format!("{table}.")))
            })
        });
        self.catalog = catalog;
        self.identity = after;
        self.accept_open();
        Ok((generation, result))
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — a key-bound proof must be invalidated even by direct SQL edits.
fn index_key_guards(conn: &Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let limit = crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES;
    let insert = format!("_index_keys_insert_{limit}");
    let update = format!("_index_keys_update_{limit}");
    let installed: i64 = conn.query_row(
        "SELECT count(*) FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type = 'trigger' AND name IN (?1, ?2)",
        params![insert, update], |r| r.get(0),
    )?;
    if installed != 2 {
        // A predecessor without both guards could have retained a proof across
        // physical edits. Install and revalidate in this activation transaction.
        conn.execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'", [])?;
        conn.execute_batch(&format!(
            "CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS {insert} AFTER INSERT ON i WHEN length(NEW.k) > {limit}
             BEGIN DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'; END;
             CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS {update} AFTER UPDATE OF k ON i WHEN length(NEW.k) > {limit}
             BEGIN DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'; END;"
        ))?;
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Equal prefixes are adjacent in an index. Validate uniqueness and caps
/// in one bounded pass per invariant, retaining one key rather than one
/// allocation for every group/row in a potentially large activation.
fn validate_groups(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: &str, index: &str, prefix_len: usize, max: u64, nulls_distinct: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::word_heap as heap};
    // Only encoded group keys matter. Fetching source images here would
    // recreate an unmetered 500-row payload buffer after the metered rebuild.
    let mut statement = tx.conn().prepare_cached("SELECT k FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 ORDER BY k")?;
    let mut rows = statement.query([tx.num(&format!("{table}.{index}"))?])?;
    let mut previous: Option<Vec<Value>> = None;
    let mut count = 0;
    let mut visited = 0;
    let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
        if visited % 256 == 0 {
            meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
            meter.at(&format!("adopt {table}.{index} groups"));
            meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        }
        visited += 1;
        let scratch = meter.mark();
        meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
        let key = row.get_ref(0)?.as_blob().map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
        meter.heap(heap::key_decode(key.len(), key.len()))?;
        if let Some(values) = &previous {
            meter.heap(heap::slots::<Value>(values.len()))?;
            crate::engine::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut meter, |meter, scope| {
                for value in values { value.retained_internal(scope).charge(meter, scope)?; }
                Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(())
            })?;
        }
        let mut group = crate::key::decode(key).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("invalid rebuilt index key".into()))?;
        group.truncate(prefix_len);
        if !(nulls_distinct && group.contains(&Value::Null)) {
            if previous.as_ref() == Some(&group) { count += 1; } else { previous = Some(group); count = 1; }
            if count > max { return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("cannot activate {table}.{index}: existing group exceeds {}", if nulls_distinct { "a unique key".to_owned() } else { format!("cap {max}") }))); }
        }
        meter.release_heap(scratch);
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn contains_asset_type(kind: &crate::schema::ColumnType) -> bool {
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind { Optional(inner) | List { of: inner, .. } | ListRange { of: inner, .. } => {
            contains_asset_type(inner)
        }
        Image { .. } | Video { .. } | File { .. } => true, _ => false }
}
pub(super) fn validate_assets(tx: &Tx<'_>, kind: &crate::schema::ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    use crate::assets::AssetKind;
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match (kind, value) {
        (Optional(inner), Some(value)) => validate_assets(tx, inner, Some(value)),
        (List { of, .. } | ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => { for value in values { validate_assets(tx, of, Some(value))?; } Ok(()) }
        (Image { .. } | Video { .. } | File { .. }, Some(value)) => {
            let (wanted, max, types) = kind.asset_leaf().expect("an asset leaf");
            if let Some(id) = crate::assets::asset_id(Some(value)) {
                if let Some(asset) = tx.asset(id)? {
                    if asset.kind != wanted { return Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE", message: format!("cannot activate: {id} is {}, not {}", asset.kind.word(), wanted.word()) }); }
                    if wanted == AssetKind::File && !crate::assets::file_accepts(types, &asset.content_type) { return Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE", message: format!("cannot activate: {id} is {}, which the column no longer accepts", asset.content_type) }); }
                    if asset.bytes > u64::from(max) { return Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE", message: format!("cannot activate: {id} has {} bytes; the column takes at most {max}", asset.bytes) }); }
                }
            }
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(())
    }
}
