    pub fn map_fields(&self, map: &impl Fn(&str) -> String) -> Self {
}

fn completed_drain(work: Work, stopping: bool) -> Result<Work, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if stopping {
        return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED", "clock", "clock advanced, but host shutdown interrupted the drain; inspect queued work instead of repeating the advance").guide("clock"));
    }
    Ok(work)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging grants clock control, never fixture or persona authority.
pub(super) fn staging(shared: &Shared) -> bool {
    lock(&shared.engine).store().meta("deployment_environment").ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some("staging")
}
fn clock_service(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<Option<String>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if shared.personas { return Ok(None); }
    if !staging(shared) { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
    match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
        Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Ok(Some(principal)),
        _ => Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "staging clock control requires a full service token").guide("clock")),
    }
}
pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    let service = match clock_service(&request, shared) {
        Ok(service) => service,
        Err(why) => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})),
    };
    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))})),
    };
    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
        None => false,
        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
    };
    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());
    let milliseconds = match duration.ok_or_else(|| crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))
        .and_then(crate::dev_clock::duration) {
        Ok(milliseconds) => milliseconds,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // Serialize clock callers only. Background slices check advancing after
    // engine admission and skip during this synchronous drain.
    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
    let _advance = lock(&advance);
    let _advancing = Advancing(shared);
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — simulated time makes retries due without stealing a live worker's claim or viewer session.
    let advanced = (|| {
        // Match claims' engine-then-clock lock order: no claim can land
        // between shifting its lease and publishing the advanced clock.
        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
        let before = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);
        if let Some(principal) = &service {
            if engine.store().meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.refusal())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
            super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&engine,principal)?;
        } else { shared.dev_authority(&engine)?; }
        let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock);
        let mut next = clock.clone();
        let now = next.advance(milliseconds)?.max(shared.time_audience_clock.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire));
        let boundaries = crate::dev_clock_store::crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary)?;
        lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing = true;
        engine.advance_worker_leases_at(milliseconds, clock.now()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
        *clock = next;
        let offset = clock.offset();
        let skew = now - snapback4_core::now_ms();
        let isolated = if crate::dev_clock_store::has_data(&engine) {
            format!("; use {} for isolated time tests; clock advance --port N targets its printed port (guide jobs)", crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())
        } else { String::new() };
        let message = format!("cumulative offset {offset:+}ms (requested process offset; now includes the authoritative floor); process offset lasts until restart{isolated}");
        Ok((now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries))
    })();
    let (now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries) = match advanced {
        Ok(result) => result,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — a developer is awaiting this
    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
    let drained = (|| {
        let mut crossed = Vec::new();
        if each_boundary {
            for (index, at) in boundaries {
                crossed.extend(engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(shared.now(), index, at))?);
            }
        }
        let mut work = work_at(shared, true, 0, Some(now))?;
        work.schedules += crossed.len();
        work.fired.extend(crossed);
        Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(work)
    })();
    match drained.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|work| completed_drain(work, crate::lifecycle::stopping())) {
        Ok(work) => {
            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary, shared.dev_identity.as_ref().map(|i| i.address.port()));
            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules}))
        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    }
}

/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
    let service=if service_route {
        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
        }
    } else {
        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
        None
    };
    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
    };
    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
    let program = match program {
        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
    };
    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
        for (field, value) in fields {
            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
            }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub query_wait: WorkHook,
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub reader_open: WorkHook,
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub changes_refresh: WorkHook,
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub mutation_wait: WorkHook,
}

impl Shared {
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#4-development-doors — current adopted auth policy closes test authority, independently of dev mode.
    pub(crate) fn dev_doors(&self) -> bool {
        if !self.personas { return false; }
        let bound = lock(&self.bound);
        self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests)
    }
    // Revalidate under the writer: neither a slow body nor an adoption queue
    // may carry fixture authority into a newly password-only schema.
    pub(crate) fn dev_authority(&self, engine: &Engine) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
        if self.personas && (self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(engine.schema().auth.password && !engine.schema().auth.guests)) { Ok(()) }
        else { Err(dev_doors::closed()) }
    }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 { lock(&self.clock).now().max(self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire)) }
    pub(crate) fn refresh_time_audience_clock(&self, engine: &Engine, now: i64) {
        if engine.schema().tables.values().any(|table| table.not_before.is_some()) {
            let durable = engine.store().meta("time_audience_clock").ok().flatten()
                .and_then(|value| value.parse::<i64>().ok()).unwrap_or(i64::MIN);
            self.time_audience_clock.fetch_max(now.max(durable), Ordering::AcqRel);
        } else {
            self.time_audience_clock.store(i64::MIN, Ordering::Release);
        }
    }
    fn advance_time_audiences(&self) -> Result<i64, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        let requested = lock(&self.clock).now();
        // Non-timed reader traffic must retain its independent reader lane,
        // including while adoption or a mutation deliberately holds the writer.
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(requested); }
        let mut engine = lock(&self.engine);
        let before = engine.seq()?;
        let now = engine.advance_time_audiences_at(requested)?;
        self.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, now);
        if engine.seq()? != before { self.durable_committed(&engine); }
        Ok(now)
    }
    fn next_time_audience(&self) -> Result<Option<i64>, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(None); }
        lock(&self.engine).next_time_audience_at()
    }
    fn time_audience_delay(&self, at: i64) -> Duration {
        let raw = lock(&self.clock).now();
            ..
        }
    );
    let hold_schedules = replay
        || matches!(
            mode,
            Mode::Dev {
                hold_schedules: true,
                ..
            }
        );
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — only immutable store classification admits a release test clock.
    let staging = if let Mode::Release { owner, .. } = &mode {
        owner.open_store(Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() == Some("staging")
    } else { false };

        dev || staging,

        },
    )
    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;

        Some(project.write_generated(&bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?)
    } else {

    };

        project.check_server_at(&mut source_stamp)?;
    }

    // Release startup already owns this lock before selecting its source.
    let mut host_lock = if matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { fresh: false, .. }) {
        Some(crate::store_owner::host(&project.store_path())?)
    } else {
        None
    };
    // A busy port is a failed start, not permission to change the stopped host's policy.
    let server =
        crate::witness_socket::server(bind, port, inherited, capacity.connections(), || {
            crate::listen_refusal::in_use(bind, port, if dev { "dev" } else { "serve" })
        })?;
    let address = server
        .server_addr()
        .to_ip()
        .ok_or("HTTP listener has no IP address")?;
    if let Some(file) = &mut host_lock {
        crate::store_owner::announce(file, address)?;
    }
    if let Mode::Release { owner, .. } = &mut mode {
        // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — publish through the acquired handle, including a release database symlink.
  return host.then(host => ({ ...host, personas: host.personas!, now: async () => {
    const { response, body } = await fetchIdentity(`${host.url}/dev/status`, deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 10_000));
    if (!response.ok || typeof body.now !== "number") throw Error("proof dev clock unavailable; password-only dev requires explicit --unsafe-dev-doors for clock/fixture tools; use serve staging for real users");
    return body.now;
  } }));
}
export interface ServeHost extends ProofHost {
  /** Authoritative staging time; requires a staging store and local operator authority. */
  now(): Promise<number>;
  /** Advance staging time once and drain due work. A lost reply must not be retried. */
  advance(duration: string, options?: { eachBoundary?: boolean }): Promise<number>;
  operatorToken(service?: string, days?: number): Promise<string>;
  seedAccounts(options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions>;
  sessions?: StagingSessions;
}
export interface ServeOptions extends ProofOptions { seedUsers?: SeedAccountsOptions; clockOffset?: string }
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — operator authority stays in the existing local store proof; bulk setup refuses production.
/** @throws E_PROOF_USAGE for missing deployment directory; child exit, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. */
export async function startServe(deployDir: string, options: ServeOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<ServeHost> {
  if (typeof deployDir !== "string" || !deployDir.trim()) throw Object.assign(new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: startServe(deployDir, options) requires a deployment directory as its first argument"), { code: "E_PROOF_USAGE" });
  const opts: ServeOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ServeOptions;
  const directory = resolve(opts.cwd ?? ".", deployDir);
  if (opts.clockOffset !== undefined && (typeof opts.clockOffset !== "string" || !/^[+-]?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(opts.clockOffset))) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: clockOffset is a signed duration such as +25h");
  if (opts.clockOffset !== undefined && opts.args?.some(arg => arg === "--clock-offset" || arg.startsWith("--clock-offset="))) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: pass clockOffset once");
  const host = await nativeHost("serve", [directory], { ...opts, args: [...(opts.args ?? []), ...(opts.clockOffset === undefined ? [] : ["--clock-offset", opts.clockOffset])] });
  const clockCall = async (body?: object): Promise<number> => {
    const config = await fetchIdentity(`${host.url}/auth/config`, deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 10_000));
    if (!config.response.ok || config.body.environment !== "staging") throw Error("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY: clock controls require a deploy --staging store");
    const clockToken = served.operatorToken("clock");
    const response = await fetch(`${host.url}/dev/clock/${body ? "advance" : "status"}`, { method: body ? "POST" : "GET", headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${await clockToken}`, "Content-Type": "application/json" }, ...(body ? { body: JSON.stringify(body) } : {}), signal: AbortSignal.timeout(deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 60_000)) });
    const result = await response.json() as { now?: number; denied?: { code?: string; message?: string } };
    if (!response.ok || result.denied || !Number.isSafeInteger(result.now)) throw Error(`staging clock refused or reply unavailable; inspect clock and queued work before retrying: ${result.denied?.code ?? response.status} ${result.denied?.message ?? ""}`);
    return result.now!;
  };
  const served: ServeHost = { ...host,
    now: () => clockCall(),
    advance: (duration, options = {}) => {
      if (typeof duration !== "string" || !/^\+?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(duration)) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: advance requires a positive whole duration");
      return clockCall({ duration, eachBoundary: options.eachBoundary ?? false });
    },
    operatorToken: async (service = "operator", days = 1) => {
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(days) || days < 1 || days > 3650) throw Error("proof operatorToken: invalid service or days (1..3650)");
      const result = await cli(["token", "--service", service, "--days", String(days), "--release", directory, "--quiet"], opts);
      if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof operator token refused: ${result.stderr}`);
      const token = result.stdout.trim();
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$/.test(token)) throw Error("proof operator token malformed");
      return token;
    },
    seedAccounts: accountOptions => seedAccounts(served, accountOptions),
  };
  try { if (opts.seedUsers) served.sessions = await served.seedAccounts(opts.seedUsers); return served; }
  catch (error) { await served.stop(); throw error; }
}

  try {

//! Row authority and dormant cell provenance are separate facts.
// @ref LLP 3000.014.000#implementation-proof-clarifications — validate the immediate manual policy before using older live evidence.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::through::{PARENT_DEADLINE, DEADLINE};

pub(super) fn mode(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<Option<u8>,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(None);}
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT CASE kind WHEN 'source-manual' THEN 0 WHEN 'source-captured' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind IN ('source-manual','source-captured','source-live') AND t=?1 AND col='' AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?)
}
pub(super) fn record(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(());}
    let kind=match &old.through {None=>"source-manual",Some(e) if e.live.is_some()=>"source-live",Some(_)=>"source-captured"};
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES(?1,?2,'',?3)",params![kind,t,id])?;
    if let Some(edge)=old.through.as_ref().filter(|e|e.live.is_none()) {
        for col in edge.fields.keys().map(String::as_str).chain([PARENT_DEADLINE,DEADLINE]) {
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES('source-capture',?1,?2,?3)",params![t,col,id])?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn live_cell(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,col:&str)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND live_source=1)",params![t,id,col],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
/// Captured-only restoration preserves its old audience. A manual row with
/// live-origin inputs must prove its *current* audience before recapture.
pub(super) fn validate(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,name:&str,old:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64,narrowed:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let Some(table)=next.table(name) else {return Ok(())};
    let t=tx.num(name)?;let id=row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
    if needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,narrowed)? {
        crate::engine::through_activation::forward_authority(tx,next,sources,name,old,row,now,true)?;
        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES('live-authority',?1,'',?2)",params![t,id])?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
/// Never let unused live cells replace a captured row's own audience/deadline.
/// Missing live-origin inputs remain absent until the capture barrier fills
/// just those new inputs from the current parent.
pub(super) fn overlay(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:Option<&Table>,table:&Table,row:&mut Value,values:Vec<(String,String)>)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let captured=old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).filter(|e|e.live.is_none());
    for (col,data) in values {
        if !table.columns.contains_key(&col) {continue;}
        let protected=captured.is_some_and(|e|e.fields.contains_key(&col) || matches!(col.as_str(),PARENT_DEADLINE|DEADLINE));
        if protected {continue;}
        let stale=captured.is_some() && live_activation::active(tx)? && live_cell(tx,t,id,&col)?;
        if let Value::Row(fields)|Value::Map(fields)=row {
            if stale {fields.remove(&col);} else {fields.insert(col,serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?);}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn live_input(table:&Table,column:&str)->bool {
    table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some() && (e.fields.contains_key(column) || matches!(column,PARENT_DEADLINE|DEADLINE)))
}
/// Current policy inputs are refreshable; the immutable parent lifetime and
/// authored references remain bound even when old policy targets disappear.
pub(super) fn obsolete_ref(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,column:&str,table:&Table,old:Option<&Table>)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    let Some(edge)=&table.through else {return Ok(false)};
    if column==PARENT_DEADLINE || !(edge.fields.contains_key(column) || column==DEADLINE) {return Ok(false);}
    let protected=if mode(tx,t,id)?.is_some() {
        tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind='source-capture' AND t=?1 AND id=?2 AND col=?3)",params![t,id,column],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?
    } else {old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_none() && (e.fields.contains_key(column) || column==DEADLINE))};
    if protected {return Ok(false);}
    Ok(edge.live.is_some() || live_activation::active(tx)? && live_cell(tx,t,id,column)?)
}

/// Reserve declared parent scratch only for callbacks that can fetch it.
/// Captured-only restoration keeps its existing actual-image admission.
pub(super) fn forward_fetch(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:Option<&Table>,table:&Table)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    match old {Some(old)=>needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,false),None=>Ok(false)}
}
/// One predicate for actual parent fetches and their preflight reservation.
fn needs_proof(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table,table:&Table,narrowed:bool)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? || old.through.is_some() {return Ok(false);}
    let Some(edge)=&table.through else {return Ok(false)};
    if edge.live.is_some() {return Ok(true);}
    for col in edge.fields.keys().map(String::as_str).chain([PARENT_DEADLINE,DEADLINE]) {
        if live_cell(tx,t,id,col)? {return Ok(true);}
    }
    if !narrowed {return Ok(false);}
    let history:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND live_source=0)",params![t,id,PARENT_DEADLINE],|r|r.get(0))?;
    Ok(!history)
}
pub(super) fn live_row(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table,table:&Table)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(false);}
    if old.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some()) || table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some()) {return Ok(true);}
    for col in table.columns.keys() {if live_cell(tx,t,id,col)? {return Ok(true);}}
    needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,true)
}
//! File-backed, transaction-local restoration identities for live transitions.
// @ref LLP 3000.014.000#71-dormant-restoration-identities-are-a-relation — never construct a population-sized id vector.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn configure(conn: &Connection, next: &Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let generations: bool = conn.query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_generations')",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?;
    let previous = if generations {
        conn.query_row(
            "SELECT schema FROM _generations ORDER BY generation DESC LIMIT 1",
            [],
            |r| r.get::<_, String>(0),
        )
        .optional()?
        .map(|s| serde_json::from_str::<Schema>(&s).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string())))
        .transpose()?
    } else {
        None
    };
    let mut dormant_live = false;
    for (table, path) in [
        ("_dormant_tables", "$.through.live"),
        ("_dormant_columns", "$.capture_source.through.live"),
        ("_dormant_narrow_rows", "$.through.live"),
    ] {
        let exists: bool = conn.query_row(
            "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",
            [table],
            |r| r.get(0),
        )?;
        if exists {
            dormant_live |= conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM {table} WHERE json_extract(definition,?1) IS NOT NULL)"),[path],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?;
        }
    }
    if super::dormant::live_cells_column(conn)? {
        dormant_live |= conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE live_source=1)",[],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?;
    }
    if dormant_live
        || crate::schema::live::has_live(next)
        || previous.as_ref().is_some_and(crate::schema::live::has_live)
    {
        conn.execute_batch("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS temp._live_restored;")?;
        conn.pragma_update(None, "temp_store", "FILE")?;
        conn.execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _live_restored(kind TEXT NOT NULL,t INTEGER NOT NULL,col TEXT NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(kind,t,col,id)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    } else {
        conn.execute_batch("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS temp._live_restored;")?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn active(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_temp_schema WHERE name='_live_restored')",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?)
}
pub(super) fn clear_stale(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if active(tx)? {
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%'",[])?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn finalize(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if !active(tx)? {
        return Ok(());
    }
    // Declaration-sized iteration, never restored-row identities.
    let declarations: Vec<(String, i64, String)> = tx
        .conn()
        .prepare("SELECT DISTINCT kind,t,col FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind IN ('table','column','narrowed') ORDER BY kind,t,col")?
        .query_map([], |r| Ok((r.get(0)?, r.get(1)?, r.get(2)?)))?
        .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
    let mut narrowed = 0_u64;
    for (kind, t, col) in declarations {
        let name: String =
            tx.conn()
                .query_row("SELECT name FROM _catalog WHERE num=?1", [t], |r| r.get(0))?;
        let count:u64=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM temp._live_restored x JOIN r ON r.t=x.t AND r.id=x.id WHERE x.kind=?1 AND x.t=?2 AND x.col=?3",params![kind,t,col],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?;
        let path = if col.is_empty() {
            name
        } else {
            format!("{name}.{col}")
        };
        tx.set_meta(
            &format!("activation_{kind}_restored:{path}"),
            &count.to_string(),
        )?;
        if kind == "narrowed" {
            narrowed += count;
        }
    }
    tx.set_meta("activation_narrowed_restored", &narrowed.to_string())?;
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn cleanup(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    tx.conn()
        .execute_batch("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS temp._live_restored;")?;
    Ok(())
}
//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
use super::*;
use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};

pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
}

/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    loop {
        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        let page_mark = meter.mark();
        let mut limit = PAGE;
        let mut page = loop {
            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
                    bytes = next;
                    count += 1;
                }
            }
            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
            let query = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
            }
            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
            drop(page);
            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
        };
        for (id, data) in &page {
            let row_mark = meter.mark();
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
        }
        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn fixture(count: usize, payload: usize) -> Store {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        for n in 0..count {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(payload))),
            ]));
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        tx.commit().unwrap();
        store
    }

    #[test]
    fn callback_keeps_the_whole_fetched_page_reserved() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let mut serialized_bytes = 0;
        for n in 0..20 {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(1024))),
            ]));
            serialized_bytes += (id.len() + data.len()) as u64;
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        let mut calls = 0;
        let mut first_heap = None;
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |_, row, meter| {
            let mut decoder = Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort);
            adoption::charge(&mut decoder, adoption::decode_bytes(&canonical_json(&row)))?;
            let decode = decoder.used.heap_bytes;
            // Independent lower bound: Rust-owned images and both native
            // payload copies coexist with decode. This excludes headers/slots,
            // so it does not just repeat the walker's allocation formula.
            assert!(meter.used.heap_bytes >= 3 * serialized_bytes + decode,
                "Rust and native page copies must coexist with decoder scratch: {:?}", meter.used);
            let expected = first_heap.get_or_insert(meter.used.heap_bytes);
            assert_eq!(meter.used.heap_bytes, *expected, "earlier rows release only their own scratch");
            assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, 20 + calls + 1, "all admission decodes plus callback decodes are charged");
            calls += 1;
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(calls, 20);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn heap_retries_keep_admission_examinations_at_the_callback_boundary() {
        let mut store = fixture(5, 1280 << 10);
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let work = adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 1, index_entries: 1 };
        let scratch = |row: &Value| adoption::contribution_bytes(row, &crate::schema::public_table(Default::default(), Default::default()));
        let mut observed = Vec::new();
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, Some(&scratch), |_, row, meter| {
            observed.push(meter.used.examined);
            assert_eq!((meter.used.steps, meter.used.index_entries), (0, 0), "rejected attempts perform no contribution work");
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            meter.step(1)?;
            adoption::charge(meter, scratch(&row))?;
            meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, 1)?;
            assert!(meter.heap_peak <= Ceilings::job().heap_bytes);
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        // Four two-image attempts each decode one row before the heap check
        // asks for a singleton. Its admission and callback decode add two.
        // The last source is already a singleton and needs no rejected attempt.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

# LLP 3000.000.000.001: Staging with real users

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 deployment, password identity, development doors, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (STAGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-18
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Proof kit](./3000.011.003.000.000-packaged-proof-kit.rfc.md), [Store ownership](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md#34-p8-residency-you-can-see-and-a-cli-that-does-not-fight-the-dev-server)

## 1. Commission and boundary

Implementer: STAGE, 2026-10-18, commissioned by Charlie's wave-16 bakeoff
fixes #1/#4. Ten Muse probes used dev because password-role provisioning
had no single recipe. This amendment supplies that recipe using the existing
signup and service-seat authority. No system seed door is added to serve.
SEC owns self-promotion lints and security invariants; KIT4 owns unrelated
proof helpers, PROD2 owns production starter adaptation.

`deploy <dir> --staging` classifies a **new deployment store**, under its
exclusive owner, with durable metadata `deployment_environment=staging`.
An existing store with no classification is production, including legacy stores;
it cannot be relabelled. A staging store remains staging through deploy,
rollback, restart, copying and source edits. There is no promote/relabel flag:
production requires a separate fresh deployment without --staging and real
operator provisioning. --check never creates the classification. Repeating
--staging on an already staging store is allowed, including while serve runs;
it verifies the existing classification without changing it. Malicious edits by
the filesystem/database owner are outside the threat model, as for service tokens.
This is a deployment-lifetime boundary stronger than a mutable per-release flag:
production source or a seed plan cannot opt a production store into test setup.

## 2. Real staging accounts

`GET /auth/config` reports `development` independently of door availability,
`devDoors`, and `environment` (`development`, `staging`, or `production`).
Missing/unknown environment metadata is production. serve remains bearer-only
in both environments, with the same password verifiers, session lifetimes,
rules, rate limits, clock and data plane. No password hashing or grant bypass.

`startServe(dir, options)` additionally returns `operatorToken(service =
"operator", days = 1)` and `seedAccounts(options)`. Minting uses the existing
local CLI proof protocol, captures secret stdout in memory and never writes
tokens into proof logs. It confers only that service seat's existing authority.
Tokens may also be requested on production for first-admin setup.

`seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` creates N real password accounts per
role, then calls the declared mutation as service:operator; `args(account)`
maps the account to application arguments (default `{principal, role}`).
`provision` is a generated mutation handle or `{name}`, including qualified names such as `accounts.provision`. The returned records
contain email, password, principal, token and expiresAt, grouped by role.
`startServe(dir, {seedUsers: options})` performs the same setup before returning
and exposes those records as `sessions`. Before minting or creating accounts,
the helper requires environment=staging and development=false. Production,
legacy and dev hosts refuse. Inputs are validated before effects; signup is
sequential, bounded, respects identity throttling, and never retries an ambiguous
write. A fresh call creates a fresh random email/password set. Partial failure
does not claim rollback: some accounts/grants may exist; discard the disposable
staging deployment or inspect it before retrying. Application data is created
through ordinary authenticated mutations after provisioning.

Credential records are returned to the operator, never logged by the helper.
The tested guide writes them to an exclusively created 0600 file. They must
not enter source control or reports. There is no public provisioning HTTP
endpoint beyond the application's declared rule-checked mutation.

## 3. First admin

The tested guide uses password signup (which grants no role), then the
filesystem owner's `operatorToken()` to invoke an operator-only mutation.
The roster's insert rule independently requires service:operator. No "first
caller wins", empty-roster bypass, client-selected signup role, or permanent
operator credential in the frontend. The same recipe works on production
with explicit operator-selected email/password; bulk seedAccounts refuses there.
Privileged account tokens cannot mint service tokens. SEC owns lints for
self-bootstrapped global authority; the guide points to this ownership.

## 4. Development doors

For password-only schemas (`auth.password && !auth.guests`), dev disables
`/seed`, every `/dev/*` door (except the existing cryptographically protected
local token protocol), and persona headers by default. The pre-existing serve
GET /dev/latency inspection remains service-authenticated; it never admits an
unauthenticated caller and its reset remains refused. `dev
--unsafe-dev-doors` is explicit opt-in with a prominent warning; serve accepts


## 2026-10-19 — MIG staging clock control

Classified staging stores admit serve --clock-offset and the existing clock
advance/status wire under a full service token. Production refuses offsets
before listener startup and all HTTP clock control even with operator authority.
The writer rechecks classification/session before advancing. startServe exposes
clockOffset, now and advance; it mints a current staging-clock operator session
for each control rather than caching a token across arbitrarily large jumps.
Fixture/reset/persona/job dev doors remain closed. The native-backed client
regression passed, including CLI advancement and restart dropping the offset.
The installed-package staging qualifier now exercises the same clock helpers.
LLP 3000.000.000.002 records the combined MIG language/adoption amendment;
independent review continues before final acceptance.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG declarative backfills and device bytes

Backfills are typed scalar patches evaluated before candidate narrowing, with
per-lifetime preimages and declaration membership retained through dormancy.
The existing activation transaction stages every result, checks historical
lookup uniqueness, then publishes data/indexes/derived state and generation.
Whole-table and narrowed restoration join the source snapshot. Hot adoption
and exact previews sample the serving clock, including staging offsets.

The web schema omits server-only migration metadata and evidence. Locked Linux
measurement is 2,385,285 B raw / 726,681 B gzip, +4,796 raw / +679 gzip versus
Integration 17. The small deterministic text library must use the same Rust
interpreter on server and device; the artifact allowance moves to measured
727,181 B (exactly 500 B headroom). No runtime meter ceiling changes. Family
and absent-feature audits and final qualification are recorded in the MIG
report; this measurement alone does not claim those checks have passed.

2026-10-19 MIG final wasm: metered text argument accounting measures 2,385,447 raw / 726,623 gzip bytes; budget tightened to 727,123 (measurement +500). Server-only backfill metadata remains absent from web wasm.

**NOT READY**

1. **[P1] Removing the last backfill can revive expired rows.** [narrowed.rs:237](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/narrowed.rs:237) retires ordinary expired sources only when the candidate contains backfills. Start with v1 without expiry; adopt v2 adding expiry and `n → n * 2`; after expiry, select v1. Retirement skips the row, reversal restores `n`, and removing expiry makes it visible again. Retire predecessor-expired lifetimes before leaving declarations too, including restored images. Add this rollback regression.

2. **[P1] Saved reference inputs lack their own parent-lifetime protection.** [backfills.rs:126](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:126) checks the dormant column ledger, while reconstruction can recover a different reference from receipt inputs at [line 262](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:262). Apply a lookup backfill using parent `p`, retarget the live input to `q`, then drop that input while carrying the declaration. Dormant evidence now protects `q`, but the receipt retains `p`. Delete/reuse `p`, remove/reintroduce the child table, and re-entry can read the replacement `p` under system authority. Bind receipt reference inputs to parent lifetimes and validate those bindings during reconstruction; cover retarget/drop/delete/reuse.

3. **[P2] Re-entry interprets historical absence using later defaults.** [backfills.rs:263](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:263) removes originally absent outputs, then [line 131](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:131) materializes them using the restored table’s newer definition. For v1 without `n`, let v2 add `n: int default 100` and `backfill f(n: int ?): {n: (n ?? 0) + 1}`. Initial adoption produces `1`; removing and reintroducing the table reconstructs `n` as `100` and produces `101`. Preserve adoption-time interpretation for absent fields across argument evaluation and lookup/index reconstruction. Add this default-bearing re-entry regression.
